190 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [JAN.-MAR.2 NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 333. OOCHOW DISTRICT. SAMSAH BAY. THE subjoined information has been received from Vice-Admiral VON DIEDERICHS, COM- mander in Chief of His Imperial German Majesty&#39;s Naval Forces on the East Asiatic Station:- &quot;NACHRICHT FUER SEEFAHRER. &quot;KREUZERGESCHWADER No. i. &quot;In der Samsah Bucht, chinesische Ostkiiste, ist am I5 November 1898 durch S.M.S. Kaiser ein Stein gefunden auf 26* 37&#39; 42&quot; N. Breite. 119&#39; 46&#39; 38&quot; 0. Linge. Wassertiefe bei Niedrigwasser auf dem Stein etwa 3.5 m. Die Oberfliche des Steines hat einen Durchmesser von .wenigen Metern; rund um denselben finden sich im Abstand von 20 In. schon Tiefen von to bi% 17 m. Die Lage des Steines fillt auf der englischen Admiraliitskarte No. 1,988 unmittelbar unter die Tiefenziffer o. &quot; v. DIEDERICHS, &quot;Vice-Admiral und Chef des Kreuzergeschwaders. &quot;(SAMSAH BUciT, &#39;,den i 8ten November 1898.&quot; The following is a translation of the above information:- On the 15th November 1898 a rock was discovered by &quot; S.M.S. Kaiser&quot; in Samsah Bay, on the east coast of China, in the following position:- Latitude . . . . . . . . . . . . 260 37&#39; 42&quot; N. Longitude . . . . . . . . . . . I19 46&#39; 38&quot; E. At low water the depth on the rock is about 31 mtres. The rock has a diameter of only a few mbtres and depths of from io to 17 mbtres are found all round it at a distance.of 20 mbtres. A depth of io fathoms is shown on the British Admiralty Chart No. 1,988 over this spot. eHNGI, 14th February 1899.
1899. NOTIFICATIONS. 191 No. 334. TTIENTSIN DISTRICT. NOTICE is hereby given that a depth of 14 feet at half ebb is reported by Captain G. B CORNER, of the s.s. Yungping, to have been obtained on the bank marked on the Admiralty Chart No. 1,256 as situated in latitude 38&#39; 57&#39; N. and longitude- 1180 So&#39; E. and as having 7 to 9 fathoms of water on it. SHANGHAI, i 5th February I899. No. 335. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. SOUTH CHANNEL ENTRANCE TO THE YANGTZE. CHANGE IN POSITION OF LIGHTSHIP &quot;KIuroAN &quot; AND Buoys. NOTICE is hereby given that the light-vessel Kiutoan has been shifted to mark the channel to the southward of the Middle Ground, and is now in the following position:-Hguse Island Beacon bearing N. 32* E., distant 3.8 miles. The Middle Ground Lower Buoy has been altered in character, and is now a red and black vertically striped buoy bearing a black spherical cage. The Blockhouse Shoal Buoy has been discontinued. A red and black vertically striped buoy bearing a black cylindrical cage has been placed in 19 feet of water, to mark the north-western end of the Middle Ground. From it House Island l3eacon bears S. 8 1o E., distant. 4- miles. This buoy is named the Middle Ground Upper Buoy. A red buoy 1Maring a black diamond cage has been placed in 13 feet of water, to mark the baik which forms the northern edge of the newly-formed channel to the northward of the Middle Ground. From it House Island Beacon bears N. 71jo E., distant 2 miles. This buoy is named the Kiutoan Flats Buoy. DIRECTIONS. Inward-bound tessels crossing the Flats to the south-eastward of the Middle Ground should do so with the Kiutoan light-vessel ahead bearing N. 730 W. On this course the least depth is I5 feet at low water of spring tides. All bearings and courses given are magnetic.
192 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [JAN.-MAJL, 1899.] Caution.-The existence of the wreck of the Fairy renders it unadvisable to take a more northerly course over the Flats than that recommended above. Owing to the transitional state of this neighbourhood ad the rapid changes that are now taking place, the above alterations should be looked upon as temporary only. SH.NGeAT, 24th February 1899. No. 838. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. NORTH CHANNEL ENTRANCE TO THE YANGTZE. LuIAO LIGeHHOUSE. NOTICE is hereby given that the light at Liu-chiao-chiang was exhibited for the first time at sunset on the 3rd instant. The illuminating apparatus is dioptric, occulting, of the fourth order, showing a fixed white light varied by an eclipse every 15 seconds, thus Light . .. . . ... . . . . . . ... . . 13 seconds. Eclipse. .............2 The lighthouse stands on the site marked Tide Beacon on the British Admiralty Chart No. 1,602, and the light, which is elevated 521 feet above the level of the sea, should be visible in clear weather at a distance of 12J nautical miles. The light is obscured by trees on bearings more westerly than N. 630 W. * This bearing is magneticand taken from seaward. The tower is an iron screw pile structure, 4o feet high, with a total height from the base to the lantern vane of 53 feet. The tower is painted white. Approximate position:- Latitude... .......... 310 29&#39; 54 N. Longitude. . ....... ... 1210 411o&#39;E SHANGnA, 17th March 1899.

APPENDIX 1. KOWLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE., JANUARY-MAROH 1899. 27
210 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [JAN.-MAR., (Received 19th April 1899.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, 14ra APRIL i899. SIR, I.--a. In conformity with the-instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistic&amp;amp; of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Nativetessels for the first quarter of the year 1899, January-March. b. The Custom House (L U U) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.h 3o and Hk.7a 8o per picul) commenced on the 14th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the ist July 1887. the stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capsuimoon (g * ) Changehow (k t), Fotochow ( ] YN),land Kowloon (* rs) stations. c. The word &quot;Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II. 1899.] LAPPA. 221 (Beceived 18th April 1899*) QUSTOM HOVSE, LAPPA, In APRL 1899. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Departat at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the irst quarter of the year 1899, January-March. b. The Custom House ( 4 N) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.7T 3o and Hk.?h 8o per plenl) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com- ienced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by Pe Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the rat July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lapp&amp;amp; Customs are known as the Malowchow (j M I) and Chienshan (t ii) stations, with three sub-stations, knowu as the Shekkok (5 ), Kuttai (&#39;L ), and Kwanchiap (U 1g) sub-stations. c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
